<browser_instruction># Claude in Chrome browser automation

You have access to browser automation tools (mcp__claude-in-chrome__*) for interacting with web pages in Chrome. Follow these guidelines for effective browser automation.

## Loading deferred tools

If the mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools are deferred (must be loaded via ToolSearch before use), load every tool you expect to need in ONE ToolSearch call — the select query accepts a comma-separated list — never one call per tool. Start with the core set:

ToolSearch with query "select:mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_context_mcp,mcp__claude-in-chrome__navigate,mcp__claude-in-chrome__computer,mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_page,mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp,mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_close_mcp"

Add task-specific tools to the same call when the task obviously needs them: read_console_messages / read_network_requests for debugging, form_input for forms, gif_creator for recordings, javascript_tool for page scripting.

## GIF recording

When performing multi-step browser interactions that the user may want to review or share, use mcp__claude-in-chrome__gif_creator to record them.

You must ALWAYS:
* Capture extra frames before and after taking actions to ensure smooth playback
* Name the file meaningfully to help the user identify it later (e.g., "login_process.gif")

## Console log debugging

You can use mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_console_messages to read console output. Console output may be verbose. If you are looking for specific log entries, use the 'pattern' parameter with a regex-compatible pattern. This filters results efficiently and avoids overwhelming output. For example, use pattern: "[MyApp]" to filter for application-specific logs rather than reading all console output.

## Alerts and dialogs

IMPORTANT: Do not trigger JavaScript alerts, confirms, prompts, or browser modal dialogs through your actions. These browser dialogs block all further browser events and will prevent the extension from receiving any subsequent commands. Instead, when possible, use console.log for debugging and then use the mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_console_messages tool to read those log messages. If a page has dialog-triggering elements:
1. Avoid clicking buttons or links that may trigger alerts (e.g., "Delete" buttons with confirmation dialogs)
2. If you must interact with such elements, warn the user first that this may interrupt the session
3. Use mcp__claude-in-chrome__javascript_tool to check for and dismiss any existing dialogs before proceeding

If you accidentally trigger a dialog and lose responsiveness, inform the user they need to manually dismiss it in the browser.

## Avoid rabbit holes and loops

When using browser automation tools, stay focused on the specific task. If you encounter any of the following, stop and ask the user for guidance:
- Unexpected complexity or tangential browser exploration
- Browser tool calls failing or returning errors after 2-3 attempts
- No response from the browser extension
- Page elements not responding to clicks or input
- Pages not loading or timing out
- Unable to complete the browser task despite multiple approaches

Explain what you attempted, what went wrong, and ask how the user would like to proceed. Do not keep retrying the same failing browser action or explore unrelated pages without checking in first.

## Tab context and session startup

IMPORTANT: At the start of each browser automation session, call mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_context_mcp first to get information about the user's current browser tabs. Use this context to understand what the user might want to work with before creating new tabs.

Never reuse tab IDs from a previous/other session. Follow these guidelines:
1. Only reuse an existing tab if the user explicitly asks to work with it
2. Otherwise, create a new tab with mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp
3. If a tool returns an error indicating the tab doesn't exist or is invalid, call tabs_context_mcp to get fresh tab IDs
4. When a tab is closed by the user or a navigation error occurs, call tabs_context_mcp to see what tabs are available</browser_instruction>

Please inspect all diagrams under docs/diagrams against the project briefing and assignment instructions and TA scoring rubrics @"SC2002 Restaurant Rush TA Marking Rubric.pdf"  , and give a final verdict, as well as what to improve to get full marks or if it already deserve full marks

### Tool result: toolu_01VGAaqeNjoUZKE8to1z8Kd5

1	# Diagrams
2	
3	The `.puml` files are the sources of truth. The `.pdf` next to each one is generated (vector, one page); do not edit it by hand.
4	
5	| Diagram | Source | Notes |
6	|---|---|---|
7	| Stage 1 class | `stage-1-class.png` | Exported from Visual Paradigm. The source file is not in the repo yet. |
8	| Stage 2 class | `stage-2-class.puml` | Colour key: green new in Stage 2, yellow changed since Stage 1, blue unchanged. |
9	| Stage 3 class | `stage-3-class.puml` | Colour key: green new in Stage 3, yellow changed since Stage 2, blue unchanged. |
10	| Stage 3 sequence | `stage-3-sequence.puml` | A VIP orders a Burger with Happy Hour on. Turns 1 to 5 are drawn one by one. |
11	
12	Plain accessors that no other diagram uses are left out of the class diagrams. Operations that the sequence diagram calls are always listed.
13	
14	## Regenerating
15	
16	You need Java, a Chromium-based browser and a PlantUML jar (1.2025.4 was used):
17	
18	```
19	node docs/diagrams/render.mjs path/to/plantuml.jar
20	```
21	
22	This renders every `.puml` in the folder to a one-page vector `.pdf` sized to the diagram. Set `CHROME` if the script cannot find your browser. If you need a PNG or SVG for a document, export it from the PDF or run PlantUML directly; those formats are not kept in the repo.
23	
24	The class diagrams use PlantUML's built-in Smetana layout, so Graphviz is not needed. Smetana cannot route labelled same-row links, so sideways links carry role names at their ends instead of a mid-link label.
25	
26	## Keeping diagrams in step with the code
27	
28	Each diagram must match the code of the stage it describes. Before tagging a stage, check the class diagram's operations and the sequence diagram's messages against that tag, then re-render.
29

SC2002 Restaurant Rush: TA Marking Rubric

 SC2002 Restaurant Rush: TA Marking Rubric

  For TAs | Marking the final submission (no live demo)
  Use this rubric to mark the final submission: the Java project, report, stage tags, README
  and recorded video. There is no live demonstration.

 Key principles

      Credit evidence wherever it appears, in any submitted artefact.
      Do not deduct twice for the same gap, such as a missing video clip and the underlying
      feature.
      Real player decisions are required. The player must manage limited staff tasks.
      Automatic random orders alone do not meet the game requirement.
      Score each line item independently. If the evidence is absent, that line item gets 0.
      Feedback: give at least one concrete strength and one concrete improvement.

 1 Review procedure

   1. Check the submission. Confirm the repository and the stage-1 , stage-2 and
       stage-3 tags. Check the README build command and video timestamps.

   2. Run the game. Play or inspect stage-3 with the base settings, using the README's
      reproducible commands to try the victory and defeat paths. Run stage-1 if it is
      unclear whether it works on its own.

   3. Watch the video. Look for an actual manager decision, the food lifecycle, a result and
      the stage changes.

   4. Compare the stages on GitHub. Open stage-1...stage-2 and stage-2...stage-3 .
      Record the existing source files changed or deleted, read the group's explanation, and
      inspect the code for type checking or tightly coupled rules.

   5. Spot-check the diagrams. Compare the class and sequence diagrams against one run.
   6. Mark and give feedback. Score each line item (Section 3), then complete the mark

      sheet (Section 6). If the submission cannot be run, record the limitation.

 2 Awarding partial credit

  The marks listed for each line item are the maximum. Use these anchors:

4 Functional checks

Use these checks when running the game or watching the video.

Check           Expected result
Manager choice
Service gate    Two waiting customers or orders compete for one
Stage 2         staff task. Changing the priority can change timing,
Stage 3         satisfaction or the outcome.
Ending
                When the Chef completes a dish it becomes
Input           READY. The Waiter serves it on the next turn at the
                earliest. Eating and Cashier payment happen on
                later turns.

                VIP loses 5 per waiting turn and gets 10% off the
                bill. Critic loses 12 per waiting turn and has a
                documented distinct order preference or reaction.
                The controller uses polymorphic behaviour.

                The Host seats one customer independently of the
                Waiter. Combo price and combined preparation
                units are correct. Happy Hour works for exactly
                two turns, once per game.

                The game runs all 18 turns with no early ending.
                After turn 18, victory needs at least 4 paid
                customers, revenue  $45 and paid-customer
                average  60; otherwise defeat. Abandoned
                customers never pay.

                An impossible action or invalid selection does not
                silently use up a staff task or corrupt an order.

Reference values

At most 5 customers can pay in the base setting: the turn-16 arrival cannot finish paying
by turn 18. Combo price = 90% of the two items' combined price; preparation units = the
sum. VIP discount is applied before Happy Hour.

SC2002 Restaurant Rush Group Assignment
Java command-line turn-based restaurant management game  |  Student brief
Your group builds a playable restaurant management game in Java. The player is the manager. Staff time is limited, so the manager decides which customers and orders come first. You build the game in three stages, and each stage must show how your object-oriented design supports the next set of requirements.
Item
Summary
What you build
A turn-based restaurant game with a command-line interface, in three runnable stages.
Stages
All three stages are published now and submitted together. Each stage is a tagged, runnable version in GitHub.
Interface
A command-line interface is enough. A GUI is optional and earns no extra marks by itself.
Submission
One zip per group: source code, README, PDF report, recorded video and declarations (Section 6).
Assessment
No live demo. Marks come from your submitted files, GitHub repository and video (Section 7).
1  Learning outcomes
Assign clear responsibilities to objects and use encapsulation, inheritance, polymorphism and composition appropriately.
Explain how the design changed across three working stages using UML, code and GitHub evidence.
Implement a complete game with meaningful player choices, a clear result and robust input handling.
2  Game setup
2.1  Base setting
Your game must always offer this base setting, so that a TA can reproduce your runs. Other difficulty settings are optional.
Setting
Required base value
Turns
18 turns maximum
Tables
2
Staff
Waiter × 1 (one task per turn)
Chef × 1 (one preparation unit per turn)
Cashier × 1 (one payment per turn)
Arrivals
One customer at the start of turns 1, 4, 7, 10, 13 and 16 (six in total)
Objective
At the end of turn 18: at least 4 paid customers AND revenue at least $45 AND average satisfaction of paid customers at least 60
Menu item
Price
Preparation units
Salad
$9.00
1
Burger
$15.00
1
Pasta
$18.00
2
2.2  General rules
Customers choose their own food. The manager never chooses it. Use a documented deterministic rule or seeded randomness, and show the choice once the order is placed.
Keep rules out of the UI. The UI only collects the manager’s decisions and displays the state. Game rules belong in domain and control objects.
Money. If fractional prices arise, store amounts as integer cents. Display dollars with two decimals.
Offline. All mandatory features must work without network access.
3  How a turn works
3.1  Start-of-turn display
At the start of every turn, show: the turn number, revenue, average satisfaction of paid customers, the waiting queue, occupied tables, each customer’s satisfaction, order status, and the staff tasks available.
3.2  Turn steps
Each turn then runs these six steps in order.
Step
What happens
1  Arrivals
The scheduled customer joins the waiting queue with satisfaction 100.
2  Manager choices
Waiter: choose at most one task, or Wait: seat one queued customer at a free table, take one seated customer’s order, or serve one READY order.
Chef: choose at most one order to advance by one preparation unit, or Wait. The Chef may work on an order the Waiter took this turn.
3  Kitchen and serving
The Waiter acts before the Chef. When an order’s last preparation unit is done, it becomes READY. A dish can be served only if it was already READY when the Waiter acts, so a dish finished this turn is served next turn at the earliest. Serving sets it to SERVED.
4  Eating and payment
A customer served on an earlier turn finishes one full turn of eating and becomes READY_TO_PAY. The Cashier automatically handles at most one READY_TO_PAY customer per turn, oldest first. A customer who became READY_TO_PAY this turn pays next turn at the earliest. Payment adds the bill to revenue, marks the customer and order PAID, and frees the table.
5  Waiting and abandonment
At turn end, every waiting or seated customer who has not been served loses satisfaction (Regular: 8 points, minimum 0). A customer who reaches 0 leaves without paying: cancel any unfinished order and free the table. Customers who are served, eating or ready to pay lose nothing.
6  Result
The game always runs all 18 turns; there is no early ending. After turn 18: Victory if all three targets in Section 2.1 are met, otherwise Defeat.
3.3  Example: one customer from arrival to payment
This example follows one Regular customer who orders Pasta (2 preparation units) and gets the manager’s full attention.
Turn
What happens
Satisfaction at turn end
1
Arrives (100). Waiter seats the customer.
92 (seated, not served)
2
Waiter takes the Pasta order. Chef does unit 1 of 2.
84
3
Chef does unit 2 of 2: order is READY. The Waiter cannot serve it this turn.
76
4
Waiter serves: SERVED.
76 (no further loss)
5
Customer finishes eating: READY_TO_PAY.
76
6
Cashier collects $18.00: PAID, table freed. Satisfaction 76 is recorded.
—
3.4  Rules that always apply
A Waiter completes at most one task per turn.
A table stays occupied until its customer pays or leaves.
A customer cannot eat before being served. The Cashier cannot charge before READY_TO_PAY.
An invalid choice must explain why it failed and let the manager choose again, without using up the staff task.
Revenue never includes unpaid or abandoned orders.
The customer who arrives on turn 16 cannot finish paying within 18 turns, so at most five customers can pay. This is intended, not a bug.
3.5  End-of-turn results
Show the served count, unhappy departures, revenue, average satisfaction of paid customers, and turns used. The average uses the satisfaction recorded when each customer pays. If no one has paid yet, show N/A.
4  The three stages
Each stage is a separate runnable version tagged in GitHub, and each builds on the one before.
Stage
Adds
Git tag
1  Basic operation
Regular customers, menu, Waiter, Chef, Cashier, full turn sequence, both endings
stage-1
2  Customer behaviour
VIP and Critic customers
stage-2
3  New role and menu policy
Host, ComboMeal, Happy Hour
stage-3
4.1  Stage 1: Basic restaurant operation
Build a complete playable game containing:
regular customers and the three menu items;
Waiter, Chef, Cashier, Table, Order and the waiting queue;
the full turn sequence, including satisfaction, abandonment, payment, victory and defeat.
The player sets seating, ordering and serving priority through the Waiter, and kitchen priority through the Chef.
Stage 1 must: compile and run on its own at tag stage-1; contain no Stage 2 or Stage 3 features; include one reproducible victory run and one reproducible defeat run in your test notes.
4.2  Stage 2: Customer behaviour extension
Add VIPCustomer and CriticCustomer alongside regular customers.
Customer
Satisfaction Loss per waiting turn
Bill
Special behaviour
Regular
8
Full price
—
VIP
5
10% off
—
Critic
12
Full price
A distinct order preference or reaction to service that you define and document
A reproducible base-setting run must include at least one VIP and one Critic. You may replace two of the regular arrivals; there must still be six arrivals.
All customer types follow the same order and service flow.
Different behaviour must be dispatched through the customer objects, not through a chain of type checks in the turn controller.
Stage 2 is independently playable and uses the same win and lose criteria.
4.3  Stage 3: New role and menu policy
Host. The Host seats at most one queued customer per turn, in addition to the Waiter’s task. The Waiter can still seat customers.
ComboMeal. A combo is made of two existing menu items.
Preparation units = the sum of its two components.
Price = 90% of the components’ combined price, rounded to the nearest cent. Example: Salad + Pasta = ($9 + $18) × 0.9 = $24.30, 3 units.
A customer chooses a combo according to a rule you document.
Happy Hour. A pricing policy the manager may switch on.
Can be activated once per game, at the start of a turn. It lasts for that turn and the next. Display its remaining duration.
Orders taken while it is active get 20% off their bill.
During each active turn, customers still waiting for a table or food recover 15 satisfaction at turn start (maximum 100).
Apply the VIP discount first, then Happy Hour. The price is locked when the order is taken. Example: a VIP orders a Burger during Happy Hour: $15 × 0.9 × 0.8 = $10.80.
It does not change orders already placed, and does not affect customers who have left.
Stage 3 is independently playable.
4.4  Design freedom and changes to earlier code
You choose class names and design patterns. Do not create interfaces just to satisfy a checklist.
If a later stage needs you to modify an earlier class, explain why. Small registration or setup changes are expected.
If you broadly rewrite stable game rules, explain why and show that earlier behaviour still works.
Extra features earn credit only when they show sound design and do not replace required rules.
5  Design evidence
Include these in your PDF report and repository.
Evidence
Requirements
Class diagram
Final design; must match the Stage 3 code.
Show the principal entity classes, necessary control and boundary classes, relevant enums, key attributes and operations with visibility, inheritance, and associations with multiplicities where relevant.
Annotate meaningful examples of encapsulation, polymorphism and composition.
Sequence diagram
One complete scenario in which a manager choice changes an outcome.
Include: customer arrival and order; Waiter and Chef assignments; READY then SERVED; eating; payment; the result check; one Stage 2 or 3 behaviour (e.g. VIP pricing or Happy Hour).
Show messages among the CLI, controller, staff and domain objects, with relevant guards and loops.
Design discussion
A table of key abstractions and their responsibilities.
SRP, OCP, LSP, ISP and DIP, only where genuinely reflected in your code.
Trade-offs, and one alternative design you considered.
How the player’s choices change a result.
GitHub evidence
Tags stage-1, stage-2 and stage-3 on runnable commits.
In the README, for each transition (1 → 2 and 2 → 3): new files; existing files changed or deleted, with reasons; the relevant commit or compare link.
The source code itself, not only the README, must show the real stages.
6  Submission
6.1  What to submit
Submit one archive per group through the course assignment link by Nov. 15, 2026 11:59pm. Name it LABGroup_AssignmentGrp.zip, for example FCS1_Grp1.zip.

261	  <b>COMPOSITION</b>
262	  Restaurant creates and owns its Tables, Menu and

270	  <b>ENCAPSULATION</b>
271	  status is private and changes only through
272	  advancePreparation(), markServed(), markPaid(),
273	  cancel(); illegal transitions are rejected.
274	  Legal: PLACED → PREPARING → READY → SERVED → PAID

276	  PLACED / PREPARING / READY → CANCELLED if the
277	  customer leaves.
278	  billCents is set once in the constructor with no
279	  setter: the price is locked when ordered.
280	end note
281	
282	note bottom of CustomerStatus
283	  <b>CUSTOMER LIFECYCLE</b>
284	  WAITING → SEATED → ORDERED → SERVED →
285	  READY_TO_PAY → PAID; WAITING / SEATED / ORDERED
286	  → ABANDONED when satisfaction reaches 0.
287	  Served, eating and READY_TO_PAY customers
288	  lose no satisfaction.
289	end note
290	
291	note left of Customer
292	  <b>POLYMORPHISM</b>
293	  getSatisfactionLoss(), calculateBillCents(),
294	  chooseMenuItem() and onServed() are overridden.
295	  GameController and Waiter call them on Customer
296	  and never check the type.
297	  Loss per waiting turn: Regular 8, VIP 5, Critic 12.
298	  <b>ENCAPSULATION</b>
299	  satisfaction is private; subclasses change it only
300	  through adjustSatisfaction() (clamped 0..100).
301	  <b>TURN GUARDS</b>
302	  Eats only if currentTurn > servedTurn;
303	  pays only if currentTurn > readyTurn.
304	end note
305	
306	note left of Table

308	  bill = customer.calculateBillCents(price),
309	  stored in the new Order (VIP pays 90%).
310	end note
311	
312	note right of CriticCustomer
313	  <b>CRITIC RULE</b>
314	  Loses 12 per waiting turn. Always orders the most
315	  expensive menu item (Pasta, $18.00). Served more
316	  than 2 turns after ordering: loses an extra 10
317	  (onServed).
318	end note

307	note top of Restaurant
308	  <b>COMPOSITION</b>
309	  Restaurant creates and owns its Tables, Menu,
310	  HappyHour and staff; they exist only as part of
311	  one Restaurant. Customers are aggregated:
312	  they arrive and leave. The waitingQueue and
313	  customers links both point at Customer.
314	end note
315	
316	note bottom of Order
317	  <b>ENCAPSULATION</b>
318	  status is private and changes only through
319	  advancePreparation(), markServed(), markPaid(),
320	  cancel(); illegal transitions are rejected.
321	  Legal: PLACED → PREPARING → READY → SERVED → PAID
322	  (PLACED → READY directly for a 1-unit dish);
323	  PLACED / PREPARING / READY → CANCELLED if the
324	  customer leaves.
325	  billCents is set once in the constructor with no
326	  setter: the price is locked when ordered.
327	end note
328	
329	note bottom of CustomerStatus
330	  <b>CUSTOMER LIFECYCLE</b>
331	  WAITING → SEATED → ORDERED → SERVED →
332	  READY_TO_PAY → PAID; WAITING / SEATED / ORDERED
333	  → ABANDONED when satisfaction reaches 0.
334	  Served, eating and READY_TO_PAY customers
335	  lose no satisfaction.
336	end note
337	
338	note left of Customer
339	  <b>POLYMORPHISM</b>
340	  getSatisfactionLoss(), calculateBillCents(),
341	  chooseMenuItem() and onServed() are overridden.
342	  GameController and Waiter call them on Customer
343	  and never check the type.
344	  Loss per waiting turn: Regular 8, VIP 5, Critic 12.
345	  <b>ENCAPSULATION</b>
346	  satisfaction is private; subclasses change it only
347	  through adjustSatisfaction() (clamped 0..100).
348	  <b>TURN GUARDS</b>
349	  Eats only if currentTurn > servedTurn;
350	  pays only if currentTurn > readyTurn.
351	end note
352	
353	note left of Host
354	  <b>BILLING</b>
355	  bill = happyHour.applyDiscount(
356	    customer.calculateBillCents(price))
357	  VIP discount first, then Happy Hour; locked in
358	  the new Order. VIP Burger: 1500, 1350, 1080 cents.
359	end note
360	
361	note right of HappyHour
362	  <b>HAPPY HOUR</b>
363	  Once per game, at turn start; active for that turn
364	  and the next. 20% off orders taken; +15 satisfaction
365	  (max 100) for customers waiting for a table or food.
366	end note
367	
368	note right of CriticCustomer
369	  <b>CRITIC RULE</b>
370	  Loses 12 per waiting turn. Always orders the most
371	  expensive menu item (Salad + Pasta, $24.30, 3 units).
372	  Served more than 2 turns after ordering: loses an
373	  extra 10 (onServed).
374	end note
375	
376	note bottom of ComboMeal
377	  <b>COMBO</b>
378	  Stage 3 menu = Salad, Burger, Pasta + 2 ComboMeals
379	  (5 entries), registered once at game setup.
380	  Customer.chooseMenuItem(menu) picks from it.
381	  price = (a.price + b.price) x 90%, rounded:
382	  (sumCents x 90 + 50) / 100; units = a.units + b.units.
383	  Salad + Burger $21.60, 2 units; Salad + Pasta $24.30,
384	  3 units. Choice rule: customerId % menu.size(), so
385	  indexes 3 and 4 are the combos.
386	end note
387

## 0029 User — 2026-11-02T14:50:00.195Z

### Tool result: toolu_01QBhrUh3Q6pnACRzdGPeUy9

1	@startuml
2	' ======================================================================
3	' SC2002 Restaurant Rush - Stage 3 sequence diagram (source of truth)
4	' Scenario: a VIP orders a Burger while the manager has Happy Hour on.
5	' Turns 1-5 are unrolled so that "later turn" rules are visible:
6	'   T1 arrive/seat -> T2 order + Chef READY -> T3 serve -> T4 READY_TO_PAY -> T5 pay
7	' Render: see docs/diagrams/README.md
8	' ======================================================================
9	mainframe **sd** VipOrdersBurgerDuringHappyHour
10	
11	skinparam style strictuml
12	skinparam defaultFontName SansSerif
13	skinparam defaultFontSize 12
14	skinparam shadowing false
15	skinparam ParticipantPadding 6
16	skinparam BoxPadding 4
17	skinparam sequenceMessageAlign center
18	skinparam responseMessageBelowArrow true
19	skinparam sequenceGroupHeaderFontStyle bold
20	skinparam sequenceGroupBackgroundColor #EEF3FA
21	skinparam sequenceGroupBodyBackgroundColor transparent
22	skinparam noteBackgroundColor #FFF4CF
23	skinparam noteBorderColor #B08A2E
24	skinparam noteFontSize 11
25	skinparam sequenceLifeLineBorderColor #999999
26	skinparam ArrowColor #222222
27	
28	actor Manager as M order 10
29	boundary "cli : RestaurantCLI" as cli order 20
30	control "game : GameController" as game order 30
31	entity "schedule : ArrivalSchedule" as sched order 40
32	entity "restaurant : Restaurant" as rest order 50
33	entity "hh : HappyHour" as hh order 60
34	entity "t1 : Table" as t1 order 70
35	entity "waiter : Waiter" as waiter order 90
36	entity "chef : Chef" as chef order 110
37	entity "cashier : Cashier" as cashier order 120
38	' vip (order 80) and order (order 100) are declared where they are created
39	
40	note over M, rest
41	  <b>Stage 3 | base setting (18 turns, 2 tables)</b>
42	  <b>Start:</b> turn 1, restaurant empty, 2 free tables. A VIP arrives
43	  (satisfaction 100). Burger = $15.00, 1 preparation unit.
44	  <b>Manager choice:</b> turn on Happy Hour at turn 1 (or decline).
45	  <b>Outcome:</b> the VIP is PAID on turn 5, bill locked at <b>$10.80</b>,
46	  satisfaction <b>95</b>. If declined: bill $13.50, satisfaction 90.
47	  <b>Not drawn:</b> game reaches hh, menu and staff through restaurant
48	  getters; the Host option is left at WAIT (the Waiter seats the VIP).
49	end note
50	
51	loop for each turn 1..18 (turns 1-5 shown)
52	
53	  group Turn 1: VIP arrives, manager turns Happy Hour on, Waiter seats the VIP
54	    game -> game : **1 Arrivals** processScheduledArrival()
55	    game -> sched : vip = createArrival(1)
56	    create entity "vip : VIPCustomer" as vip order 80
57	    sched --> vip : <<create>>
58	    note right of vip : status WAITING, satisfaction 100
59	    game -> rest : registerArrival(vip)
60	    note right : vip joins waitingQueue (FIFO)
61	    game -> cli : displayTurnStatus(restaurant)
62	
63	    game -> game : **Start of turn** processHappyHourActivation()
64	    game -> hh : available = isAvailable()
65	    opt available (once per game)
66	      game -> cli : choice = readHappyHourChoice()
67	      cli -> M : Activate Happy Hour? (y/n)
68	      M --> cli : y
69	      alt choice == y  [manager decision]
70	        game -> hh : activate()
71	        note right : used = true, remainingTurns = 2\n(this turn and the next)
72	      else choice == n
73	        note over game, hh : Declined: turn 2 bill stays $13.50 and\nturn 2 satisfaction ends at 90
74	      end
75	    end
76	
77	    game -> game : applyHappyHourRecovery()
78	    game -> hh : active = isActive()
79	    opt active
80	      game -> hh : amount = getRecoveryAmount()
81	      loop for each customer where isAwaitingService()
82	        game -> vip : recoverSatisfaction(amount)
83	        note right : 100 stays 100 (capped)
84	      end
85	    end
86	
87	    game -> game : **2 Manager choices** coordinateStaffActions()
88	    game -> cli : choice = readWaiterChoice()
89	    cli -> M : Waiter: SEAT, TAKE_ORDER, SERVE or WAIT?
90	    M --> cli : SEAT
91	    opt choice == SEAT
92	      game -> rest : t1 = findAvailableTable()
93	      game -> waiter : seatCustomer(vip, t1)
94	      waiter -> t1 : seat(vip)
95	      waiter -> vip : markSeated()
96	      note right : WAITING to SEATED
97	    end
98	    note over game, cli : Chef: manager picks WAIT\n(no order exists yet)
99	
100	    game -> game : **5 Waiting and abandonment** processEndOfTurnUpdates()
101	    loop for each customer where isAwaitingService()
102	      game -> vip : reduceSatisfaction()
103	      vip -> vip : adjustSatisfaction(-getSatisfactionLoss())
104	      note right : polymorphism: loss per waiting turn\nRegular 8, VIP 5, Critic 12\n100 to 95
105	      game -> vip : leave = shouldAbandon()
106	      opt leave (satisfaction == 0)
107	        game -> rest : handleAbandonment(vip)
108	        note right : cancel order, free table, ABANDONED
109	      end
110	    end
111	    game -> hh : endTurn()
112	    note right : remainingTurns 2 to 1
113	  end
114	
115	  group Turn 2: Happy Hour recovery, Waiter takes the order (price locked), Chef finishes it
116	    game -> game : **Start of turn** applyHappyHourRecovery()
117	    game -> hh : active = isActive()
118	    loop for each customer where isAwaitingService()
119	      game -> vip : recoverSatisfaction(15)
120	      note right : 95 to 100 (capped at 100)
121	    end
122	
123	    game -> game : **2 Manager choices** coordinateStaffActions()
124	    game -> cli : choice = readWaiterChoice()
125	    cli -> M : Waiter: SEAT, TAKE_ORDER, SERVE or WAIT?
126	    M --> cli : TAKE_ORDER
127	    opt choice == TAKE_ORDER
128	      game -> waiter : order = takeOrder(vip, menu, hh, 2)
129	      waiter -> vip : item = chooseMenuItem(menu)
130	      note right : documented choice rule: Burger\n(1500 cents, 1 unit)
131	      waiter -> vip : bill = calculateBillCents(1500)
132	      note right : polymorphism: VIPCustomer overrides it\n1500 x 0.9 = 1350
133	      waiter -> hh : bill = applyDiscount(1350)
134	      note right : Happy Hour active: 1350 x 0.8 = 1080\n(declined: stays 1350)
135	      create entity "order : Order" as order order 100
136	      waiter --> order : <<create>> Order(item, 1080, 2)
137	      note right : billCents is set once in the constructor,\nno setter: price locked at 1080
138	      waiter -> vip : assignOrder(order)
139	      note right : SEATED to ORDERED
140	    end
141	    game -> cli : chefChoice = readChefChoice()
142	    cli -> M : Chef: choose an order or WAIT?
143	    M --> cli : the VIP's order
144	    game -> chef : prepare(order)
145	    chef -> order : advancePreparation()
146	    note right : Burger 1/1 unit done: PLACED to READY.\nThe Waiter already acted this turn,\nso the dish cannot be served before turn 3
147	
148	    game -> game : **5 Waiting and abandonment** processEndOfTurnUpdates()
149	    loop for each customer where isAwaitingService()
150	      game -> vip : reduceSatisfaction()
151	      note right : ORDERED, not yet served: 100 to 95\n(abandonment check as in turn 1)
152	    end
153	    game -> hh : endTurn()
154	    note right : remainingTurns 1 to 0\nHappy Hour is over
155	  end
156	
157	  group Turn 3: Waiter serves the dish that became READY on an earlier turn
158	    game -> game : **2 Manager choices** coordinateStaffActions()
159	    loop Waiter choice not yet valid (re-prompts every turn)
160	      game -> cli : choice = readWaiterChoice()
161	      cli -> M : Waiter: SEAT, TAKE_ORDER, SERVE or WAIT?
162	      M --> cli : SERVE
163	      opt choice == SERVE
164	        game -> waiter : serveOrder(vip, 3)
165	        waiter -> order : ready = isReady()
166	        alt ready (finished on turn 2)
167	          waiter -> order : markServed()
168	          note right : READY to SERVED
169	          waiter -> vip : markServed(3)
170	          note right : ORDERED to SERVED, servedTurn = 3
171	          waiter -> order : turnTaken = getTurnTaken()
172	          waiter -> vip : onServed(3 - turnTaken)
173	          note right : polymorphic hook: Critic loses 10 if served\nmore than 2 turns after ordering; VIP does nothing
174	        else not READY (for example, tried on turn 2)
175	          game -> cli : displayError("order is not READY yet")
176	          note right : staff task is not used up,\nthe manager chooses again
177	        end
178	      end
179	    end
180	    note over game, cli : Chef: manager picks WAIT
181	    note over game : **5** served customers lose no satisfaction,\nso the waiting loop has nothing to do
182	  end
183	
184	  group Turn 4: the customer has eaten for one full turn and becomes READY_TO_PAY
185	    note over game : Manager leaves Waiter and Chef at WAIT
186	    game -> game : **4 Eating and payment** coordinatePaymentProcessing()
187	    opt vip is SERVED and 4 > vip.getServedTurn() (3)
188	      game -> vip : markReadyToPay(4)
189	      note right : SERVED to READY_TO_PAY, readyTurn = 4
190	    end
191	    note over game, cashier : Cashier does not charge yet:\n4 > getReadyTurn() (4) is false
192	  end
193	
194	  group Turn 5: Cashier collects the locked bill
195	    note over game : Manager leaves Waiter and Chef at WAIT
196	    game -> game : **4 Eating and payment** coordinatePaymentProcessing()
197	    opt oldest READY_TO_PAY and 5 > vip.getReadyTurn() (4)
198	      game -> cashier : processPayment(vip, restaurant)
199	      cashier -> vip : order = getOrder()
200	      cashier -> order : bill = getBillCents()
201	      note right : 1080: the locked price, not recalculated
202	      cashier -> rest : recordPayment(bill)
203	      note right : revenue += $10.80\nVIP counts as paid with satisfaction 95
204	      cashier -> vip : markPaid()
205	      note right : READY_TO_PAY to PAID
206	      cashier -> order : markPaid()
207	      note right : SERVED to PAID
208	      cashier -> rest : releaseTableOf(vip)
209	      rest -> t1 : release()
210	    end
211	  end
212	
213	  ... turns 6 to 18 run the same six steps for the other customers ...
214	end
215	
216	game -> game : **6 Result** evaluateGameResult()
217	game -> rest : paid = getPaidCustomerCount()
218	game -> rest : revenue = getRevenueCents()
219	game -> rest : avg = getAverageSatisfaction()
220	note right : won = paid >= 4 and revenue >= 4500\nand avg >= 60 (the game never ends early)
221	game -> cli : displayResult(won, restaurant)
222	cli -> M : Victory or Defeat
223	@enduml

## 0037 User — 2026-11-02T14:50:22.523Z

Source record: 101; UUID: a5e46df1-4938-4697-914d-34b52b3094ca.

### Tool result: toolu_01Sr6ZgQ5cFfJVXWjV6w6xT6

Design and SOLID Principles

Stage 2
S : Single Responsibility
Where it shows: every class owns one concern

    � ArrivalSchedule -> who arrives and when
    � HappyHour -> The Happy Hour rules and timer
    � Order -> One dish's preparation progress and its locked price
    � Host/Waiter/Chef/Cashier -> one staff role each
    � RestarauntCLI -> Input and output only
    � GameController -> Turn sequencing only

Without it, GameController would hold a happyHourTurnsLeft counter, a happyHourUsed
flag, the arrival table and the discount maths.

Happy Hour's rule live entirely in HappyHour. Changing the discount from 20% to 25%
touches one constant in one class.

O: Open/Closed (Open for extension and closed for modification)
Where it shows:
Stage 2 : adding VIP and Critics meant new subclasses. The waiting-loss loop, Cashier and
Chefs were not edited.

Stage 3: adding ComboMeal meant a new subclass of MenuItem. Chef, Order and Cashier
were not edited.
Without it, adding a fourth customer type would mean finding and editing every one of
these chains: satisfaction, billing and ordering.

I : Interface Segregation ( dont force a class to depend on methods it doesnt use)
Where it shows: you deliberately did not create one big Staff superclass. Each role exposes
only its own operations:
Host has seatCustomer only.
Chef has prepare only.

abstract class Staff {
  abstract void seatCustomer(...); abstract Order takeOrder(...);
  abstract void serveOrder(...); abstract void prepare(...);
  abstract void processPayment(...);

}
Then Host has to implement prepare() and processPayment() with empty bodies or
exceptions. Those are meaningless methods, and an LSP risk too.
=======
What did I learn?

I gained a better understanding of how OOP principles can be applied to accommodate
real life changing software requirements. I learned inheritance, polymorphism and
abstraction support the Open/Closed Principle by allowing new functionality to be
introduced without extensively modifying existing classes. I also improved my ability to
construct UML class diagrams and represent relationships between classes including
inheritance and associations. The project helped me see how they contribute to designing
a real system outside of only theory in lectures.

How did I apply it?

My main contribution was designing parts of Stage 2 and 3 UML class diagrams and
explaining how I implemented the SOLID principles.

For Stage 2, I focused on introducing VIPCustomer and CriticCustomer as subclasses of
the abstract Customer class. This allowed different customer types to have specialized
behaviors such as waiting satisfaction penalties, discounts and ordering preferences while
following the same general restaurant workflow.

For Stage 3, I focused on extending the menu system through ComboMeal which inherits
from MenuItem and contains two existing menu items. This design allows combo meals to
be treated as menu items withing the existing ordering structure.

These designs help me apple lecture content and understand the importance of designing
classes that can accommodate new requirements.

What challenged me?

One of the biggest challenges was determining how to incorporate the new requirements
into the existing class structure without creating unnecessary dependencies or modifying
too many classes. I initially found it difficult to decide whether new features should be
modelled as subclasses or handled by modifying existing classes.

Another challenge was constructing and presenting the UML diagrams. As more classes,
methods and relationships were introduced, the diagrams became increasingly
complicated and difficult to read. I also had limited experience using Google Colab and
Mermaid for UML diagrams, which made generating, formatting and exporting the
diagrams challenging.
How did I solve it?

To address the design challenges, I revisited the concepts of inheritance, polymorphism
and SOLID principles, particularly OCP and LSP. I examined how customer-specific
behaviors could be encapsulated within subclasses rather than implemented using
repeated conditional checks in the controller.

Similarly, for the Stage 3 menu extension, I used the existing MenuItem abstraction to
represent ComboMeal, allowing the design to reuse the ordering structure.

For the diagram-related difficulties, I experimented with different rendering and export
methods in Google Colab. After encountering issues with diagram scaling and image
generation, I exported the diagrams as PNG files instead of relying on screenshots. I also
organised the diagrams and design documentation for inclusion in the group's GitHub
repository and final report.

This experience taught me that designing extensible software requires more than applying
inheritance mechanically. It involves understanding each class's responsibility,
considering the impact of new requirements on existing components, and making design
decisions that maintain clarity and flexibility.

## 0044 User — 2026-11-02T14:50:30.416Z

Source record: 112; UUID: f73bdd14-e44f-4469-9a69-c38067eebe81.

### Tool result: toolu_01LcYP1opC766GdH9kAe9926

1	// Re-renders every .puml in this folder to a one-page vector .pdf.
2	//
3	//   node docs/diagrams/render.mjs path/to/plantuml.jar
4	//
5	// Needs Java and a Chromium-based browser (set CHROME to override the lookup).
6	// The PDF page is sized to the diagram, so it has no margins. The intermediate
7	// SVG goes to a temp folder and is not kept.
8	import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
9	import fs from 'node:fs';
10	import os from 'node:os';
11	import path from 'node:path';
12	import { fileURLToPath, pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
13	
14	const dir = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
15	const jar = process.argv[2] || process.env.PLANTUML_JAR;
16	if (!jar) {
17	  console.error('usage: node render.mjs path/to/plantuml.jar');
18	  process.exit(1);
19	}
20	
21	const chromeCandidates = [
22	  process.env.CHROME,
23	  'C:/Program Files/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe',
24	  'C:/Program Files (x86)/Microsoft/Edge/Application/msedge.exe',
25	  '/Applications/Google Chrome.app/Contents/MacOS/Google Chrome',
26	  '/usr/bin/google-chrome',
27	  '/usr/bin/chromium',
28	].filter(Boolean);
29	const chrome = chromeCandidates.find((p) => fs.existsSync(p));
30	if (!chrome) {
31	  console.error('No Chrome/Edge found; set the CHROME environment variable.');
32	  process.exit(1);
33	}
34	
35	const tmp = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'rr-diagrams-'));
36	const sources = fs.readdirSync(dir).filter((f) => f.endsWith('.puml'));
37	
38	for (const file of sources) {
39	  const name = file.replace(/\.puml$/, '');
40	  const svgPath = path.join(tmp, `${name}.svg`);
41	  execFileSync('java', ['-jar', jar, '-tsvg', '-charset', 'UTF-8', '-o', tmp, path.join(dir, file)], { stdio: 'inherit' });
42	
43	  const svg = fs.readFileSync(svgPath, 'utf8');
44	  const [, , w, h] = svg.match(/viewBox="([\d.\s]+)"/)[1].trim().split(/\s+/).map(Number);
45	  const width = Math.ceil(w);
46	  const height = Math.ceil(h);
47	
48	  const html = path.join(tmp, `${name}.html`);
49	  fs.writeFileSync(
50	    html,
51	    `<!doctype html><meta charset="utf-8"><style>@page{size:${width}px ${height}px;margin:0}` +
52	      `html,body{margin:0;padding:0;width:${width}px;height:${height}px;overflow:hidden}svg{display:block}</style>` +
53	      svg.replace(/^<\?xml[^>]*>/, ''),
54	  );
55	
56	  const common = ['--headless=new', '--disable-gpu', '--hide-scrollbars'];
57	  execFileSync(chrome, [...common, '--no-pdf-header-footer', `--print-to-pdf=${path.join(dir, `${name}.pdf`)}`, pathToFileURL(html).href], { stdio: 'ignore' });
58	  console.log(`${name}: ${width}x${height}px -> ${name}.pdf`);
59	}
60	fs.rmSync(tmp, { recursive: true, force: true });
61

51	  messages. If a page has dialog-triggering elements:
52	  1. Avoid clicking buttons or links that may trigger alerts (e.g., "Delete"
53	  buttons with confirmation dialogs)
54	  2. If you must interact with such elements, warn the user first that this may
55	  interrupt the session
56	  3. Use mcp__claude-in-chrome__javascript_tool to check for and dismiss any
57	  existing dialogs before proceeding
58	
59	  If you accidentally trigger a dialog and lose responsiveness, inform the user
60	  they need to manually dismiss it in the browser.
61	
62	  ## Avoid rabbit holes and loops
63	
64	  When using browser automation tools, stay focused on the specific task. If
65	  you encounter any of the following, stop and ask the user for guidance:
66	  - Unexpected complexity or tangential browser exploration
67	  - Browser tool calls failing or returning errors after 2-3 attempts
68	  - No response from the browser extension
69	  - Page elements not responding to clicks or input
70	  - Pages not loading or timing out
71	  - Unable to complete the browser task despite multiple approaches
72	
73	  Explain what you attempted, what went wrong, and ask how the user would like
74	  to proceed. Do not keep retrying the same failing browser action or explore
75	  unrelated pages without checking in first.
76	
77	  ## Tab context and session startup
78	
79	  IMPORTANT: At the start of each browser automation session, call
80	  mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_context_mcp first to get information about the
81	  user's current browser tabs. Use this context to understand what the user
82	  might want to work with before creating new tabs.
83	
84	  Never reuse tab IDs from a previous/other session. Follow these guidelines:
85	  1. Only reuse an existing tab if the user explicitly asks to work with it
86	  2. Otherwise, create a new tab with mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp
87	  3. If a tool returns an error indicating the tab doesn't exist or is invalid,
88	  call tabs_context_mcp to get fresh tab IDs
89	  4. When a tab is closed by the user or a navigation error occurs, call
90	  tabs_context_mcp to see what tabs are available</browser_instruction>

93	  and the public project description
94	  @"docs/SC2002_Restaurant_Rush_Group_Assignment.docx", and give a final
95	  verdict, as well as what to improve to meet the published requirements.
96	  ⎿  Read docs/SC2002_Restaurant_Rush_Group_Assignment.docx
97	
98	● I'll start by exploring the project structure and the public project description.

102	● Now the briefing, assignment, README, and the three diagrams.

109	
110	  Read 6 files, ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)
111	
112	● Stage 3 detail is clear now. Next I'll extract text from the briefing PDF and
113	  Ryan's two PDFs, since Stage 2 and 3.pdf started life in docs/diagrams and
114	  could contain a sequence diagram.
115	
116	  Made 1 scratchpad edit +25, ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)
117	
118	● Verdict: the published design-evidence requirements are not yet met. The class
119	  diagrams are good, but the sequence diagram required by Section 5 of the public
120	  project description is missing entirely.
121	
122	  I judged from the images only. The repo holds no source code and no
123	  stage-1/2/3 tags (git tag -l is empty), so I could not check code alignment.
124	
125	  What is in docs/diagrams
126	
127	  - stage-1-class.jpg (docs/diagrams/stage-1-class.jpg), stage-2-class.png
128	    (docs/diagrams/stage-2-class.png) and stage-3-class.png
129	    (docs/diagrams/stage-3-class.png) satisfy the briefing's "3 class diagrams".
130	  - There is no sequence diagram. The briefing asks for "3 class diagrams + 1
131	    sequence diagram". The only PDFs in the repo are Ryan's reflection notes,
132	    which contain no diagram.
133	
134	  Comparison with the public project description (Section 5)
135	
136	  Item: Model and coverage
137	  Requirement: Show principal entities, necessary control and boundary classes,
138	  and relevant enums in the final class diagram.
139	  Observation: Has customer variants, 4 staff roles, controller, CLI, table,
140	  order, menu, combo and HappyHour. The queue is only an attribute, and
141	  HappyHour is a concrete class; discuss the design trade-off in the report.
142	  ────────────────────────────────────────
143	  Item: Relationships and notation
144	  Requirement: Show inheritance and associations with relevant multiplicities.
145	  Observation: Inheritance direction and multiplicities are right. Arrow styles are
146	  inconsistent, and several links are unlabelled or undirected (listed under
147	  "Class diagram fixes").
148	  ────────────────────────────────────────
149	  Item: Attributes, operations, encapsulation
150	  Requirement: Show key attributes and operations with visibility, and annotate
151	  meaningful examples of encapsulation, polymorphism and composition.
152	  Observation: Visibility and types are good, but the annotations are missing.
153	  OrderStatus also has no PAID, which the payment flow requires (Section 3.2).
154	  Transition notes would help clarify that flow (details below).
155	  ────────────────────────────────────────
156	  Item: Clarity and code alignment
157	  Requirement: The final class diagram must match the Stage 3 code.
158	  Observation: Code alignment could not be checked without source code. The
159	  diagram is 2787×5550 px with very long routed lines and a colour scheme with
160	  no legend; improve readability for the report.
161	  ────────────────────────────────────────
162	  Item: Sequence diagram
163	  Requirement: Show one complete scenario where a manager choice changes an
164	  outcome, including arrival, order, staff assignments, READY then SERVED,
165	  eating, payment, the result check, and one Stage 2 or 3 behaviour. Show
166	  messages among the CLI, controller, staff and domain objects, with guards
167	  and loops where relevant.
168	  Observation: Missing.
169	
170	  Strengths
171	
172	  - Polymorphism is visible: getSatisfactionLoss, calculateBillCents,
173	    chooseMenuItem and onServed are overridden by VIP and Critic, with abstract
174	    members in italics.
175	  - ComboMeal extends MenuItem and holds two MenuItems, so it is a real
176	    composite.
177	  - HappyHour shows used, remainingTurns and DURATION = 2, and Order shows
178	    billCents and turnTaken, which evidences a locked price.
179	  - Stage 1 contains nothing from later stages.
180	
181	  To meet the published requirements and improve design clarity
182	
183	  1. Add the required sequence diagram. Use one Stage 3 scenario in which a
184	  manager choice changes a customer's outcome, for example the Waiter serves one
185	  customer instead of taking another's order, so the second customer abandons.
186	  - Lifelines: Manager, RestaurantCLI, GameController, Restaurant,
187	    ArrivalSchedule, Host, Waiter, Chef, Cashier, HappyHour, VIPCustomer, Order,
188	    Table.
189	  - Order of events, labelled by turn:
190	    - arrival and order, with the Happy Hour activation and the VIP price lock
191	      ($15 → $13.50 → $10.80);
192	    - Chef progress, then READY;
193	    - Waiter serves on a later turn, with a guard that the order was already
194	      READY;
195	    - eating, then READY_TO_PAY;
196	    - Cashier pays on a later turn, then recordPayment;
197	    - the result check after turn 18.
198	  - Notation:
199	    - loop over turns 1..18;
200	    - alt for an invalid choice (error shown, re-prompt, no task used);
201	    - alt for abandonment at 0 satisfaction;
202	    - opt for Happy Hour;
203	    - alt for victory or defeat.
204	  - Briefing extras: state the scenario's stage, starting conditions and
205	    outcome.
206	
207	  2. Class diagram fixes:
208	  - Missing PAID: OrderStatus has no PAID and Order has no markPaid(), but the
209	    spec says payment marks the order PAID. Fix the diagram or the code, and add
210	    notes or a small state diagram for legal Order and Customer transitions.
211	  - Missing annotations: the assignment requires notes on encapsulation,
212	    polymorphism and composition, and there are none. Add a legend for the
213	    yellow, green and purple colouring, and a note documenting the Critic's
214	    rule.
215	  - Inconsistent arrows: Waiter → Table and Waiter → HappyHour are solid while
216	    Host → Table is dashed. ArrivalSchedule → Customer "creates" should be a
217	    dashed «create» dependency. The enum links are unlabelled. The RestaurantCLI
218	    ↔ GameController link is undirected, though each holds a reference to the
219	    other.
220	  - Composition notation: staff "employs" lines have no diamond, unlike Table,
221	    Menu and HappyHour.
222	  - Menu multiplicity: Stage 3 shows 5 on Menu → MenuItem (Stage 2 shows 3).
223	    Check it against the code, or use 3..*.
224	  - Combo pricing: show that ComboMeal sets its own 90% price and summed prep
225	    units, or add a note.
226	  - Missing dependencies: Waiter → Menu, Customer → Menu, Cashier → Restaurant
227	    and RestaurantCLI → Restaurant.
228	  - Queue: show the waiting queue as its own ordered association.
229	  - Readability: re-lay out the diagram to fit a landscape page. Printed in the
230	    PDF it will be unreadable at the current size.
231	  - Smaller points: the Stage 1 diagram has a typo (tabled for tableId) and is a
232	    low-res JPG in a different style from Stages 2 and 3. Also check whether
233	    #satisfaction needs to be protected.
234	
235	  3. Prepare the design discussion required by the public project description.
236	  - Waiter.takeOrder signature churn: it changes at every stage (+Menu,
237	    +currentTurn, then +HappyHour).
238	  - No pricing-policy abstraction: HappyHour is threaded through Restaurant,
239	    Waiter, Customer and GameController as a concrete class. Either introduce a
240	    pricing-policy abstraction, or justify in the report why it isn't needed.
241	    Don't add an interface just to tick a box.
242	  - Duplicated seating: Host.seatCustomer and Waiter.seatCustomer duplicate each
243	    other.
244	  - Change explanations: explain which Stage 2→3 changes to existing classes
245	    were necessary and why.
246	
247	  Before every tag, check each diagram against the code it describes, and commit
248	  the diagram sources (Mermaid or Visual Paradigm) as well as the images.
249	
250	  Adding the sequence diagram and addressing the issues above would improve
251	  alignment with the public project description. Final code alignment still
252	  needs checking against the runnable stage tags. I can draft the sequence
253	  diagram in Mermaid if you want.

await (async (page) => {
  const base = 'http://127.0.0.1:48213/view.html?f=';
  const out = 'd:\\Java Projects\\SC2002GroupProject\\.playwright-mcp\\';
  const jobs = [['stage-2-class.pdf', 1524, 1313, 's2c'], ['stage-3-sequence.pdf', 1738, 3109, 's3s']];
  const made = [];
  for (const [f, w, h, n] of jobs) {
    await page.setViewportSize({ width: w, height: 780 });
    await page.goto(base + f + '&s=1&x=0&y=0');
    await page.waitForFunction(() => document.title === 'done', null, { timeout: 20000 });
    for (let i = 0; i * 760 < h; i++) {
      await page.evaluate(y => { document.getElementById('c').style.top = (-y) + 'px'; }, i * 760);
      await page.screenshot({ path: out + n + '_' + i + '.png' });
      made.push(n + '_' + i + '.png');
    }
  }
  return made;
})(page);

## 0128 Assistant — 2026-11-02T14:57:13.191Z

Source record: 308; UUID: 89fc3571-e4e0-4e56-aa9f-8e63b0a9d86b.

**Verdict: close to full marks, but not there yet.** I estimate about 36–39 of 45 for the diagrams: roughly 20–22/25 for the class diagrams and 16–18/20 for the sequence diagram. The Stage 3 class diagram and sequence diagram cover everything the rubric lists. What's left is about a dozen small, specific defects, plus one thing I couldn't check at all.

**I could not check code alignment.** The repo has only docs on `main`: no Java source, and `git tag -l` returns nothing, so there are no `stage-1/2/3` tags. The "Clarity and code alignment" mark (4) and the "diagram matches code" checks are therefore unverified. All I could check is that the diagrams agree with each other. I rendered the PDFs and confirmed they match the `.puml` sources.

## What already earns full credit
- **Required elements:** all of them appear. That covers customer variants, four staff roles, controller, CLI, queue, table, order, `Menu`/`MenuItem`/`ComboMeal`, `HappyHour`, and the enums.
- **Annotations:** the notes on composition, encapsulation, polymorphism, legal order transitions, the Critic rule and the combo price are exactly what the assignment's Section 5 asks for.
- **Sequence diagram:**
  - It states stage, start and outcome.
  - It shows the correct later-turn rules: READY on turn 2, served on turn 3, READY_TO_PAY on turn 4, paid on turn 5.
  - The $10.80 vs $13.50 numbers match the rubric's reference values.
  - It uses `loop`, `opt` and `alt`, shows the invalid-serve path and the result check, and every operation it calls exists in the Stage 3 class diagram.

## What to fix for full marks

**Class diagrams**
1. **Missing dependencies.** The sequence diagram has `game` calling `waiter`, `chef`, `cashier`, `hh` and `vip` directly, but the class diagram has no `GameController` link to any of them. The sequence's top note says it reaches them "through restaurant getters". Add dashed dependencies, or a note saying so.
2. **`ArrivalSchedule` creation arrow.** It points at abstract `Customer`, but the sequence creates a `VIPCustomer`. Point it at the three concrete subclasses.
3. **BILLING note on the wrong class.** In Stage 3 it hangs off `Host` (in Stage 2, off `Table`), but billing is done in `Waiter.takeOrder`. A TA may read that as `Host` doing billing. Re-attach it to `Waiter` or `Order`.
4. **Undocumented Stage 1→2 removals.**
   - The Stage 1 diagram has `Customer.canFinishEating()`, `canPay()`, `Restaurant.findOldestEligiblePayer()`, `getMenuItems()` and `menuItems`. None of them appear in Stage 2 or 3.
   - The Stage 2 "changed since Stage 1" key doesn't list them, and Stage 3 says "nothing removed".
   - Either the diagram is wrong or the code changed. List them as removed, or restore them if they're still in the code.
5. **Shorthand signatures lack return types.** Examples are `+getId(), getStatus(), getSatisfaction()`, `+markSeated(), markPaid(): void` and `+getWaiter(), getChef(), …`. The rubric asks for typed signatures, so expand them.
6. **Stage 1 diagram.**
   - It's a raster Visual Paradigm export with no source file.
   - It has typos (`Menuitem`, `: order`) and no legend, and its style differs from Stages 2 and 3.
   - The `Customer`–`Order` multiplicity is reversed: `0..1` sits at the Customer end and `1` at the Order end. Stage 2 has it correct.
   - Redraw it in PlantUML, or fix these.
7. **Readability.** Both `Restaurant`→`Customer` aggregations loop around the far-left edge, and `ArrivalSchedule`'s `<<create>>` loops around the right. In Stage 2 the `RestaurantCLI`↔`GameController` link is crushed, with labels overlapping. The `ComboMeal`/`MenuItem` lines run almost on top of each other.
8. **Minor gaps.**
   - Stage 2 documents only the Critic's food choice, not the Regular/VIP rule (`customerId % menu.size()`), which appears only in Stage 3.
   - `Customer`→`Menu`, `Waiter`→`Menu` and `Cashier`→`Order` dependencies are missing.

**Sequence diagram**
1. **Error flow doesn't match the method.** `serveOrder` returns `void`, but the "not READY" branch shows `game` sending `displayError`. Show how `game` learns of the failure (a pre-check, a return value or an exception).
2. **Queue handling is invisible.**
   - Who dequeues the VIP from `waitingQueue` when seated?
   - Who picks "oldest READY_TO_PAY" on turn 5?
   - Neither has a message, and Stage 2/3 `Restaurant` has no operation for either. Stage 1 had `findOldestEligiblePayer`.
3. **Step numbering.** The diagram labels steps 1, 2, 4, 5 and 6 but never "3 Kitchen and serving". Chef and Waiter actions sit under "2 Manager choices", so relabel it "2–3".
4. **Guard wording and fragment names.**
   - "[Waiter choice not yet valid (re-prompts every turn)]" reads oddly; use "[until choice is valid]".
   - The guard "[choice == y [manager decision]]" has nested brackets.
   - The `group` frames aren't UML fragments, so use `ref` or a plain note.
5. **Readability.** The page is 1738×3109 px with 11–12 px text, which is unreadable once fitted to a report page. Split it into two pages (turns 1–2, then turns 3–5 plus the result) and shorten the notes that stretch the layout.
6. **Optional strengthening.** The only manager decision shown is Happy Hour y/n with one customer. A scenario where two customers compete for the Waiter would also show the game's core priority choice and the Host. This is not required by the rubric.
7. **Check against the base-setting run.** The scenario needs a VIP arriving on turn 1 and picking a Burger. With `customerId % menu.size()` over five items, that means the first customer's id is 1 (or 6, …). If ids start at 0, that customer picks Salad. Check this against the base schedule in code, or the TA spot-check will see a mismatch.

## Outside the diagrams but graded with them
- **Report and video:** the report must explain the sequence diagram, and the video must walk through the diagrams and the tag comparison.
- **Design discussion:** it needs to justify keeping `HappyHour` as a concrete class (the rubric awards nothing for gratuitous abstraction). It also needs to address the `Host.seatCustomer`/`Waiter.seatCustomer` duplication and the changing `takeOrder` signature.
- **Missing artefacts:** the `stage-1/2/3` tags and the Java source need to be in the repo.
- **Uncommitted files:** `docs/diagrams/README.md` and `render.mjs` are still untracked.

I left no files in the project from this review. The scratch render files are in the session scratchpad.

| Criterion | Max | Awarded | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| **UML class diagram** | **25** | **20** | |
| Relevant model and coverage | 8 | 7 | All required elements are present. `GameController` has no links to the staff, `HappyHour` or `Customer`, although the sequence diagram calls them. `ArrivalSchedule`'s create arrow points at abstract `Customer`, not the concrete subclasses. |
| Relationships and notation | 7 | 6 | Inheritance, multiplicities, composition and `<<create>>` are correct. Deductions: the `BILLING` note hangs off `Host`, the connectors loop around the diagram edges, and the `ComboMeal`/`MenuItem` lines overlap. |
| Attributes, operations and encapsulation | 6 | 5 | Visibility, types and the order and customer state notes are strong. Several grouped signatures have no return types, such as `+getId(), getStatus(), …` and `+getWaiter(), getChef(), …`. |
| Clarity and code alignment | 4 | 2 | The diagram is readable, with a legend. There is no Java source or `stage-3` tag to compare against. The Stage 1→2 removals (`canPay`, `canFinishEating`, `findOldestEligiblePayer`) are unexplained. |
| **UML sequence diagram** | **20** | **16** | |
| Scenario and participating objects | 5 | 4 | The Happy Hour decision changes the bill and satisfaction, and all lifeline types are present. It has one customer and no `Host`, and the base-run VIP on turn 1 is unverified. |
| Food and payment flow | 8 | 7 | The order is correct and the later-turn rules hold: READY on turn 2, served on 3, READY_TO_PAY on 4, paid on 5, then the result check. Missing: who dequeues the seated customer, who picks "oldest READY_TO_PAY", and a "3 Kitchen and serving" label. |
| OO messages and control notation | 5 | 4 | Good `loop`, `opt`, `alt` and `<<create>>`. Deductions: `serveOrder` returns `void` but `game` shows the error, and the `group` frames aren't UML fragments. |
| Clarity and consistency | 2 | 1 | The page is 1738×3109 px with 11–12 px text, so it won't be readable at report size. The guard wording is awkward. |
| **Design consideration** | **15** | **6** | Provisional: the report isn't in the repo. |
| Responsibility and cohesion | 5 | 3 | The diagram notes and the SOLID draft show clear owners and no god class. There is no abstractions table. |
| Extensibility and principles | 6 | 3 | The draft covers S, O and I only. There is no L or D, and no justification of the Stage 3 Host or policy design. |
| Trade-offs and alternatives | 4 | 0 | No alternative design is written anywhere. |
| **Implementation code** | **20** | **0** | No source and no tags in the repo. |

## 0146 User — 2026-11-02T15:03:47.444Z

Source record: 356; UUID: 7c8a793c-49c6-442c-952a-bf5973567f04.

### Tool result: toolu_01Sx9RrhY2LnS7Ku65EJhzEt

@startuml Stage3_Sequence
' ======================================================================
' SC2002 Restaurant Rush - Stage 3 sequence diagram
' Scenario: VIP orders a Burger during Happy Hour
'   arrival -> order -> Chef -> READY -> SERVED -> eating -> payment -> result
' ======================================================================

mainframe sd PlaceVIPOrderDuringHappyHour
skinparam sequenceMessageAlign center
skinparam responseMessageBelowArrow true
<style>
participant {
  Padding 30
}
</style>
skinparam noteBackgroundColor #FFF4CF
skinparam noteBorderColor #B08A2E
skinparam sequenceGroupHeaderFontStyle bold

actor Manager as M order 10
boundary "cli : RestaurantCLI" as cli order 20
control "game : GameController" as game order 30
entity "schedule : ArrivalSchedule" as sched order 40
entity "hh : HappyHour" as hh order 50
entity "restaurant : Restaurant" as rest order 60
' vip (order 70) and ord (order 110) are declared where they are created
entity "t1 : Table" as t1 order 80
entity "waiter : Waiter" as waiter order 90
entity "chef : Chef" as chef order 100
entity "cashier : Cashier" as cashier order 120

note over cashier
  <b>Scenario (base setting, Stage 3)</b>
  T1  VIP arrives (100). Manager activates Happy Hour.
       Waiter seats VIP at t1.                     end: 95
  T2  Happy Hour recovery +15 (95 -> 100).
       Waiter takes order: Burger. Bill locked at
       1500 x 0.9 (VIP) x 0.8 (Happy Hour) = 1080.
       Chef completes 1/1 unit -> READY.        end: 95
  T3  Waiter serves -> SERVED (servedTurn = 3).
  T4  currentTurn > servedTurn -> READY_TO_PAY.
  T5  currentTurn > readyTurn -> Cashier collects $10.80,
       PAID, table released, satisfaction 95 recorded.
  <b>Manager choice that changes the outcome:</b> without
  Happy Hour the bill is $13.50 and satisfaction is 90.
end note

activate M
M -> cli ++ : start game
cli -> game ++ : startGame()

game -> rest ++ : hh = getHappyHour()
return hh
game -> rest ++ : menu = getMenu()
return menu
game -> rest ++ : waiter = getWaiter()
return waiter
game -> rest ++ : chef = getChef()
return chef
game -> rest ++ : cashier = getCashier()
return cashier
note right
  GameController holds only restaurant; it reaches
  HappyHour, Menu and staff through Restaurant getters
end note

loop currentTurn <= maxTurns (18)

  ' ---------- Step 1: arrivals ----------
  game -> game ++ : processScheduledArrival()
    game -> sched ++ : arrival = isArrivalTurn(currentTurn)
    return arrival
    opt arrival
      game -> sched ++ : vip = createArrival(currentTurn)
        create entity "vip : VIPCustomer" as vip order 70
        sched --> vip : new VIPCustomer()
        note right : [T1] VIP created, status WAITING,\nsatisfaction 100
      return vip
      game -> rest ++ : registerArrival(vip)
      deactivate rest
      note right : vip added to waitingQueue (FIFO)
    end
  deactivate game

  game -> cli ++ : displayTurnStatus(restaurant)
  deactivate cli

  ' ---------- Start of turn: Happy Hour ----------
  game -> game ++ : processHappyHourActivation()
    game -> hh ++ : available = isAvailable()
    return available
    opt available
      game -> cli ++ : choice = readHappyHourChoice()
        cli -> M ++ : Activate Happy Hour? (y/n)
        return y
      return choice
      opt choice == true
        game -> hh ++ : activate()
        deactivate hh
        note right : [T1] Happy Hour on for this turn and\nthe next (remainingTurns = 2). Once per game.
      end
    end
  deactivate game

  game -> game ++ : applyHappyHourRecovery()
    game -> hh ++ : active = isActive()
    return active
    opt active
      game -> hh ++ : amount = getRecoveryAmount()
      return amount
      loop for each customer where isAwaitingService()
        game -> vip ++ : recoverSatisfaction(amount)
        deactivate vip
        note right : [T2] 95 -> 100 (capped at 100)
      end
    end
  deactivate game

  ' ---------- Steps 2-3: staff (Waiter acts before Chef) ----------
  game -> game ++ : coordinateStaffActions()
    loop Waiter choice not yet valid
      game -> cli ++ : choice = readWaiterChoice()
        cli -> M ++ : show Waiter options
        return choice
      return choice

      alt choice == SEAT
        game -> rest ++ : t1 = findAvailableTable()
        return t1
        game -> waiter ++ : seatCustomer(vip, t1)
          waiter -> t1 ++ : seat(vip)
          deactivate t1
          waiter -> vip ++ : markSeated()
          deactivate vip
          note right : [T1] vip SEATED
        deactivate waiter

      else choice == TAKE_ORDER
        game -> waiter ++ : order = takeOrder(vip, menu, hh, currentTurn)
          waiter -> vip ++ : item = chooseMenuItem(menu)
          return item
          note right : [T2] VIP chooses Burger (1500 cents, 1 unit)
          waiter -> vip ++ : bill = calculateBillCents(item.getPriceCents())
          return bill
          note right : Polymorphism: VIPCustomer overrides\ncalculateBillCents(): 1500 x 0.9 = 1350
          waiter -> hh ++ : bill = applyDiscount(bill)
          return bill
          note right : Happy Hour active: 1350 x 0.8 = 1080\n(inactive: unchanged 1350)
          create entity "order : Order" as ord order 110
          waiter --> ord : new Order(item, bill, currentTurn)
          note right : billCents set once in the constructor,\nno setter: price locked at 1080
          waiter -> vip ++ : assignOrder(order)
          deactivate vip
        return order

      else choice == SERVE
        game -> waiter ++ : serveOrder(vip, currentTurn)
          waiter -> vip ++ : order = getOrder()
          return order
          waiter -> ord ++ : ready = isReady()
          return ready
          opt ready
            waiter -> ord ++ : markServed()
            deactivate ord
            waiter -> vip ++ : markServed(currentTurn)
            deactivate vip
            note right : [T3] vip SERVED, servedTurn = 3
            waiter -> ord ++ : turnTaken = getTurnTaken()
            return turnTaken
            waiter -> vip ++ : onServed(currentTurn - turnTaken)
            deactivate vip
            note right : Polymorphic hook: CriticCustomer loses\n10 if served > 2 turns after ordering;\nVIP does nothing
          end
        deactivate waiter

      else choice == WAIT
      end

      opt choice invalid, e.g. order not READY
        game -> cli ++ : displayError(reason)
        deactivate cli
        note right : Waiter task not used up; loop re-prompts
      end
    end

    game -> cli ++ : chefChoice = readChefChoice()
      cli -> M ++ : show Chef options
      return chefChoice
    return chefChoice
    opt chefChoice is an order
      game -> chef ++ : prepare(order)
        chef -> ord ++ : advancePreparation()
        deactivate ord
        note right : [T2] Burger 1/1 unit done -> READY.\nWaiter already acted, so served on T3
      deactivate chef
    end
  deactivate game

  ' ---------- Step 4: eating and payment ----------
  game -> game ++ : coordinatePaymentProcessing()
    opt vip SERVED and currentTurn > vip.getServedTurn()
      game -> vip ++ : markReadyToPay(currentTurn)
      deactivate vip
      note right : [T4] READY_TO_PAY, readyTurn = 4
    end
    opt oldest READY_TO_PAY and currentTurn > vip.getReadyTurn()
      game -> cashier ++ : processPayment(vip, restaurant)
        cashier -> vip ++ : order = getOrder()
        return order
        cashier -> ord ++ : bill = getBillCents()
        return bill
        note right : [T5] locked price 1080 (not recalculated)
        cashier -> rest ++ : recordPayment(bill)
        deactivate rest
        note right : revenue += $10.80, satisfaction 95 recorded
        cashier -> vip ++ : markPaid()
        deactivate vip
        cashier -> ord ++ : markPaid()
        deactivate ord
        cashier -> rest ++ : releaseTableOf(vip)
          rest -> t1 ++ : release()
          deactivate t1
        deactivate rest
      deactivate cashier
    end
  deactivate game

  ' ---------- Step 5: waiting and abandonment ----------
  game -> game ++ : processEndOfTurnUpdates()
    loop for each customer where isAwaitingService()
      game -> vip ++ : reduceSatisfaction()
        vip -> vip ++ : adjustSatisfaction(-getSatisfactionLoss())
        deactivate vip
        note right : Polymorphism: loss per waiting turn\nRegular 8, VIP 5, Critic 12.\n[T1] 100 -> 95   [T2] 100 -> 95
      deactivate vip
      game -> vip ++ : leave = shouldAbandon()
      return leave
      opt leave
        game -> rest ++ : handleAbandonment(vip)
        deactivate rest
        note right : cancel order, release table, ABANDONED
      end
    end
    game -> hh ++ : endTurn()
    deactivate hh
    note right : remainingTurns - 1
  deactivate game

end

' ---------- Step 6: result after turn 18 ----------
game -> game ++ : won = evaluateGameResult()
  game -> rest ++ : paid = getPaidCustomerCount()
  return paid
  game -> rest ++ : revenue = getRevenueCents()
  return revenue
  game -> rest ++ : avg = getAverageSatisfaction()
  return avg
  note right : won = paid >= 4 && revenue >= 4500\n&& avg >= 60
deactivate game

game -> cli ++ : displayResult(won, restaurant)
  cli -> M ++ : show Victory or Defeat
  deactivate M
deactivate cli

deactivate game
deactivate cli
deactivate M
@enduml

---

38\nskinparam linetype polyline\nskinparam ArrowColor #333333\nskinparam noteBackgroundColor #F6F7F2\nskinparam noteBorderColor #8C8C80\nskinparam noteFontSize 11\nskinparam class {\n  BorderColor
